# Plan progress — `docs/AD4_IMPROVEMENT_PLAN.md` (v2)

This file is where a new session resumes. Keep it accurate. Update it in every
PR that advances a step.

**Latest handoff: `docs/handoff-2026-09-30/` (start with `00_START_HERE.md`)** (30 Sep ~09:00Z). It answers Hassan's three questions from the live database: are we running in circles, why the predictive section trails the market, and why US cities are missing from the hit/miss view (a regression since 25 Sep, plus a table cut to 8 cities). It lists the fixes in order (F1, F2, F3) and the checks due on 1 and 5 Oct. Read it after `CLAUDE.md` and before this checklist. The 27 Sep handoff (`docs/HANDOFF_2026-09-27.md`) is superseded.

**WXPredict, the predictive core engine (Hassan, 5 Oct: "WE NEED TO BEAT THE MARKET ... APPROVED, PROCEED TO PHASE 1").** **The build is governed by `docs/WXPREDICT_BUILD.md`** (v2.2, the final build document, 5 Oct ~17:30Z, awaiting Hassan's approval; wave A, the Actions revamp, approved by Hassan: "as long as we maintain quality, don't cut corners, and improve the accuracy of the engines"; v2.2 adds wave P, the paper desks, which Hassan reported on 5 Oct are not visible or working: measured, the API answers and the page opens on an empty desk, and no paper trade has opened since 29 Sep 22:36Z because the strategies that traded were retired that day and the remaining ones made 0 BUY in 11,591 decisions since 30 Sep). It holds every open item on the platform, measured 5 Oct (section 3: minutes on course for about 3,300 in October, the database at 575 MB / 114.9%, the jobs warning, the unfinished plan steps), one order of work (waves A, 0, P and 2, phases 1-5), the full test that ends it (section 7), and Hassan's decisions (section 9). The sealed test (1 Sep - 4 Oct and later) is untouched. Resume from its status table. Everything else stops until it is built. The plan has five phases, set out in `docs/WXPREDICT.md`: (1) clean, refresh, one training table; (2) the market's price fresh at each decision; (3) the model: A weather, B buckets, C market fusion, D calibration; (4) walk-forward against the market; (5) nightly cycle, shadow and the board, with Hassan deciding serving. numpy and scikit-learn may be added to the jobs (approved).
- **Phase 1 (this PR).**
  - **Market record:** refreshed to 7 Oct (`tools/market_history.py seed`). All 9,977 old events, 108,841 buckets and 107,215 price series are unchanged, except the 143 events that closed since.
  - **Station reports:** every report of the 50 settlement stations with every field, 1 Jun 2025 - 5 Oct 2026 (902,238 rows), plus station days back to 2020 for climatology (`tools/wxpredict/fetch_obs.py`).
  - **Hourly forecast record:** extended to 4 Oct (`tools/wxpredict/fetch_forecasts.py`). The 10,368 hours are new; the 2,304 overlapping hours matched exactly.
  - **The table:** `tools/wxpredict/build_table.py` gives 686,111 rows, 9,586 listed events and 11,855 unlisted station days. Two builds gave identical bytes (sha256 `097799a77116...` in `data/training/wxpredict/table_meta.json`).
  - **Leak rules:** a report counts from valid + 20 min, measured against IEM; a forecast hour counts from H - 17 h (P2.9's assumption); the market is the hour's own snapshot, stamped 0-59 s past the hour; past days count only once whole.
  - **Label:** the venue's winner. The station's maximum agrees on 9,410 of 9,586 listed days; 118 of the 176 that disagree are Shenzhen's, Mar - Aug.
  - **Tests:** `tests/test_wxpredict_table.py`.
  - **Not done:** phases 2-5. Market activity is only a proxy: there is no volume in the record.

- **F3, Challenger C (this PR): accepted as a better same-day forecast, research only.** Pre-registered before any score (`docs/CHALLENGER_C_PREREG.md`, the result appended there). `rd3` = rd1's twelve features plus three from the seven day-before model maxima, each bias-corrected per model and city (shrunk to the pooled bias, prior 30 days, bound ±4 °C, refitted per cutoff on training days only): the mean rise above the running maximum, the share of models more than 0.5 °C above it, and the count. Untouched window (1 Aug - 25 Sep, 29,334 city-day-hours, 56 dates, 48 cities, 10 min receipt lag, `data/eval/fec_v1/sd_holdout_lag10_rd3.json`): log loss rd1 1.0942, rd3 1.0224, gain +0.0718 (90% +0.0644 to +0.0787); every hour 7-17 positive (7h +0.1415 ... 17h +0.0028); top-1 55.8% to 58.6%; 80% coverage 0.844, width 2.152 to 2.001 °C. All four rules hold. Development (Jan-Jul, 70,071 rows, 165 dates): +0.0646 (+0.0593, +0.0699). The market is still far ahead on the same rows: rd3 minus market -0.2909 (-0.3065, -0.2760), market top-1 70.0% vs 58.9% (28,017 complete-ladder rows). Next: forward shadow under its own `model_version`, not feeding any price (`fec-v1` §8); it replaces rd1 only on forward evidence. **The day-ahead lane (`tools/fec_day_ahead.py`, a frozen anon export 13-29 Sep):** 706 city-days, 17 dates, 49 cities: engine top-1 33.4%, log loss 1.769; the raw forecast's bucket 30.4%; engine minus raw top-1 +0.030 (90% -0.007, +0.067), not shown; on 693 head-to-head rows the market's top-1 is 46.9% vs the engine's 33.6% and the engine's shared-bucket Brier is worse by +0.134 (+0.119, +0.152). The priced centre beats the raw forecast on MAE by 0.137 °C (+0.048, +0.228) on the 306 rows with a recorded centre, 7 dates only (`da_report_2026-09-13_2026-09-29.json`).

The `pytest` job always runs, so a required check is never left pending (Codex on #315, P1): its first step (`tools/ci_paths.py --decide`) reads the pull request's own diff, the merge commit against its base, and every later step runs only when a changed file can reach a test. Any doubt (not a pull request, no two-parent merge, git failing) runs everything. A docs-only pull request costs about 1 billed minute instead of 4-6; a code run is unchanged. What may be skipped is `.github/ci-paths.txt`: `docs/**` and the root's `*.md`, with the 8 documents a test opens put back. It is measured: `tools/ci_paths.py --write` runs the suite with a recorder in `tests/conftest.py`, and an independent audit-hook trace found the same 8. **Child processes count too (Codex, P2):** Python and Node children inherit hooks (`tools/ci_child_hook/`) that record what they read; any other child's command line is checked for a skipped document.             - The rail, measured: decide caps a city-day at 3% of equity on cost at the fee its price carries ($30.00); the plan reserves each leg's cost at its limit plus the worst fee plus a cent, rounded up (milan 46.75 x 0.63 + 46.75 x 0.0125 + 0.01 = 30.05; tel_aviv 30.01), and the rail sums the reservations. Any BUY sized to the rail was refused whole by a few cents; the anchored strategies never met it because at w = 0 they do not buy. Fixed in this PR: `engine_orders.fit_to_room` scales every leg of a plan by one factor until its reservations fit the room decide sized it to (`room_usd`, in whole cents, down), drops a leg that falls under the venue minimum, and records `fitted_to_room` in the plan's evidence. Both refused plans fit once scaled (tests use their published legs).

The twins reached 95 of 253 (37.5%; 158 out of time, `model_only_cut_short` in 8 ticks): about the 80 a day estimated above, although the solver now runs 4.1x faster, because the spare time, not the solve, is what binds. Their share follows when the engine starts within the tick (correlation -0.78): engine start under 20 s, 6 ticks, 38 of 45 city-days (84%); 20-24 s, 9 ticks, 45 of 91 (49%); 24 s or later, 9 ticks, 12 of 117 (10%). Engine start (tick seconds less engine and variants) ranged 16.3-34.6 s, mean 23.0; the IEM observation read is 2-3 s of it and checkpoint pricing most of the rest. Tick 25.2-43.0 s, mean 35.2. Twin orders: 32 BUYs, 30 queued, fills 16 filled, 24 partial, 1 rejected. Ledgers at 9 Oct 12:52Z: s12_no_model 30 trades (6 settled, net +$65.31, cash $812.90), s11_ladder_model 7 (2 settled, -$20.60, $955.53), s10_growth_model 6 (1 settled, +$45.83, $1,020.39); s10_winner_model, s10_lock_model and s11_lock_model none. **Not changed.** The twins get what the one-minute tick leaves (their stop is 32 s in, so the 45 s deadline and one billed minute hold). Every way to give them more costs something: a later stop or a longer deadline risks a second billed minute (+24 a day, against the Actions cap); running them before the anchored six would starve the strategies that trade; a parallel process on the runner's second core is untested for what it does to the anchored pass. A self-hosted runner (no billed minutes) would let the deadline move. Put to Hassan with the Actions decision. **Changed 9 Oct (this PR), after Hassan made the repository public** (standard runners are not billed on a public repository, GitHub's billing docs read 9 Oct): `tick.py` `BUDGET_S` 45 -> 100 s and tick.yml's `TICK_DEADLINE` 48 -> 110 s, so the twins' stop moves about a minute later. Nothing in the engine changed: it already gives the twins what is left before the deadline. From Codex's review: tick.py takes its budget from the job's deadline too (`tick.budget`: the nearer of 100 s and `TICK_DEADLINE`, never under 45 s), so a slow checkout or cold install comes out of the tick instead of running past the deadline, and the job's timeout goes 3 -> 5 minutes (deadline, then the NWS steps that carry no deadline, then the trade prints' 120 s backstop: about 240 s before the NWS steps). P6.1 (rule 7): `MEASURED_MINUTES['tick.yml']` 3.0, an upper bound until measured (a run that ends at its deadline passes 120 s in the 16 hours with an NWS step); `SCHEDULED_MINUTE_BUDGET` 2,850 -> 6,000 with the reason in the constant (a guard against a runaway schedule, about one and a half times the 4,096 a month it now totals); the rule's wording in `CLAUDE.md` and the plan says the repository is public. **Acceptance:** over the next 24 ticks, `model_only_out_of_time` near 0 with the anchored `out_of_time` still 0, and the tick's seconds under 100.

        - **Taken (Hassan, 5 Oct: "stop rerunning test on main after merge"):** `tests.yml` and `web.yml` no longer run on pushes to main, only on pull requests and by hand. The PR's run is the check, and the squash lands the same tree. Pinned by `test_ci_does_not_run_again_on_main_after_a_merge`. The first lever applies from this session on; intraday's cadence is unchanged.
    - **The wrapper's first scheduled night (5 Oct, `ingest_log`):** pass 1 logged 04:46:36Z `partial`, with 3 archive chunks unreached and Milan's current run missing. Pass 2 logged 04:46:54Z `ok`: 150 archive rows and Milan's current run (21 rows). The archive was incomplete, so its continuation carried Milan.
    - **A missed current run retried on its own (audit P3, 5 Oct, this PR; Hassan: "fix the nightly run and retry gap").**
      - **The gap:** a missed current run is not `incomplete`, so a night whose archive was complete stopped after one pass and stayed green. Since 4 Oct nothing else fetches a current run, so a missed city stays on the night before's.
      - **How often** (`ingest_log`, 1-5 Oct): 10 of the 13 passes that asked for every city's current run missed 1-4 cities.
        - 3 of those had a complete archive: the 04:4x runs of 1, 3 and 4 Oct, with 4, 4 and 1 cities missed.
        - Those 9 cities still had a run from 03:44-03:50Z the same night, from the old chain (`weather_forecast_models`). On 4 Oct that was Madrid at 03:50Z.
        - On 5 Oct the first pass left the archive incomplete, so the archive's continuation carried the retry.
      - **The fix:** when the archive's passes end, by whatever rule, the wrapper asks again for the current runs of only the cities still missing.
        - Up to 2 current-only passes (`FORECAST_ARCHIVE=0`: no archive window, no coverage reads), with a 2-minute soft deadline and a 5-minute timeout.
        - None is started after 26 minutes.
        - Their results never enter the archive's verdict; a crash still fails the step.
      - **What is left is named:** the ingest's row is `partial` and lists each city still missing with the newest run it holds (`current_snapshot_of`, one 10 s read per city). The step prints a `::warning::` naming them. It does not go red: the archive's verdict is unchanged, and the next night asks again.
    - **The model inputs' freshness, tracked apart from the main forecast's (audit P3's last sentence, 5 Oct, the PR after #311; Hassan: "do the freshness tracking too").**
      - **Two clocks** (measured 5 Oct):
        - The main forecast (`weather_forecasts`) is fetched every 3.00 h for all 48 cities: 768 runs in 48 h, the largest gap 3.00 h. Each call records its age (`forecast_issued_at`).
        - The seven models' current run is fetched once a night. From 28 Sep to 5 Oct every city got one each night, with all 7 models, and the cities' newest runs were at most 0.94 h apart.
      - **The gap:** the model inputs reach pricing through `derived_corrected_forecast` (and the MOS blend that reads it). That row is stamped when the fit combined them (`computed_at`, which the engine's 36 h check reads). The fit takes each model's newest run whatever its age, so a city combined from the night before's run looked fresh. `v_data_freshness` judges each table by its newest row.
      - **Recorded:** `scripts/station_correction.py` writes `inputs_oldest_run_at` and `inputs_newest_run_at` on each corrected row, from the runs the combination used. The fit of 6 Oct is the first. Earlier rows are not backfilled: they read `not_recorded`.
      - **Judged per city:** `v_city_forecast_inputs` (`20261005110000_the_model_inputs_have_their_own_freshness.sql`).
        - **Main forecast:** the one the engine picks for today. Stale past 8 h (the freshness spec's).
        - **Model inputs:** the newest current run and its model count. Stale past 36 h (the spec's). `behind` when it is more than 6 h older than the newest any city has, meaning a missed nightly fetch (6 times the measured spread). `partial` when it holds fewer than the 7 models the ingest asks for: under 4 the correction combines nothing (review of #312).
        - **Today's and tomorrow's corrected rows:** `earlier_run` when an input was more than 6 h older than the newest run at the fit.
        - **Access:** ages and counts only. It is the owner's view (as `v_city_observation_health`), read as `anon`; `weather_forecast_models` and `derived_corrected_forecast` stay closed to `anon`.
      - **Shown:** on `/live`, under the observation panel (`CityForecastInputs`).
      - **Dry run on production** (rolled back, 08:2xZ): 48 cities; main forecasts 1.42 h old at most; model inputs 3.55–3.62 h old, 7 models each; every city fresh on both clocks. The corrected rows were judged on a reconstruction made for the dry run only (each model's newest run before the fit), and all read `current`. About 150 ms a read as `anon`.
      - **Not changed:** what prices. The engine's check still reads `computed_at`. Gating on the inputs' age would change served prices, which is Hassan's decision.
    - tests.yml runs pytest and the database contracts side by side. Every test still runs. On two cores locally the pair took 174 s. The first run on GitHub (this PR, 4 Oct) took 170 s and billed 3 minutes, where the median was 274 s billed 5.
    - /paper-trades states the cycle as the clock's (:36, every six hours); it said :15 every four hours.
  - **Budget:** `SCHEDULED_MINUTE_BUDGET` 2,850 and `MEASURED_MINUTES` from the runs above. pipeline_daily is set at 38 until its new shape is measured (29.0 plus the slowest first forecast link, 799 s, less the 276 s step it replaces). The scheduled projection is 2,835 (about 2,750 on the means), leaving 150-250 a month for CI. `SCHEDULED_RUN_BUDGET` is 1,000 (964 runs).
  - **Not verified yet:** the merged daily run's length, the new intraday cycles, the day-ahead call's age on real pricings, and CI's billing over many runs (one run measured). Re-measure after a week.
  - **CI decides whether the total holds.** 150-250 minutes covers roughly 50-80 test runs a month at 3 billed each. September's build used 749. Next levers if CI runs hot: intraday every 8 hours (-120 a month; the decisions freshness limit would move to 9 h), or fewer pushes per PR.

  - **Floor.** `v_city_running_max` and the stored `live_weather.running_max_c` took the all-source value. On 24 Sep - 2 Oct, 19 of 450 US checkpoint calls put the floor above the winning bucket (houston 1 Oct: floor 33 °C, winner 88-90 °F, probability on it 0.0006).

- **Statuses:** `todo` · `doing` · `done` · `skipped` (the finding no longer
  holds; the reason is in the notes) · `blocked` (the reason is in the notes).
- **Done** means the step's acceptance check passed against the **live**
  database or the live Actions API, and the query and its output are in the PR
  description. A green workflow is not enough.
- The plan was written against `main @ 908427c`. Its line numbers are hints.
  Re-verify every finding in the code before changing anything.
- Every number in this file names the query or command it came from.

Open-Meteo timed out on 17 of 48 cities in link 1's first pass; 11 answered the second pass and link 2 finished the other 6 (ALL CITIES COMPLETE); each link spent ~3.6 min on the current-run reads, mostly 20 s timeouts. pipeline_daily 29 min 39 s (billed 30), its forecast ingest 3 min 29 s (1,063 s on 27 Sep). archive_observations 10 min 43 s (billed 11) and FAILED: the honest training record (P2.9) ran into its 5-minute step timeout; 27 Sep's run took 6 min 45 s. `MEASURED_MINUTES` now reads forecasts 16, archive_observations 11, pipeline_daily 30: 2,926 of the 3,000-minute budget a month (was 2,956), 1,054 of 1,060 runs. |
| P5.9 | Fixed safety rails | ✅ decided 24 Sep: Hassan delegated it ("do what's the most optimal option"). Claude took the plan's defaults: 5% daily loss, 3% per city-day, 8% per cluster-day, price bound 0.97, no entry in the last 15 min, kill switch. Never learned. |
| P5.10 | Activate the portfolio account, and its bankroll | ✅ decided 24 Sep, delegated the same way: a $10,000 paper bankroll (the plan's suggestion). Activation is by evidence, not by date: a strategy is promoted to `portfolio` automatically once its shadow ledger meets the plan's gate (at least 30 settled dates, at least 60 decisions, a positive lower 80% bound on log-growth per dollar, no city-day over 25% of the gain). The account goes active when at least one strategy is in `portfolio`, with Thompson allocation and a 40% cap per strategy. Built in P5.10 (row below). |
| P8.1 | Consolidate the nine strategies into S10 / S11 / S12 / S2 / S13 | ✅ decided 27 Sep (in chat, after the 09:30Z update): Hassan approved the consolidation as proposed ("yes approve consolidation and proceed"); no old strategy kept as its own id. History and old signals are kept; s1, s3-s9 retire as ids once the new ones decide live (P8.2 step 5 after P5.12 part 3), so no shadow evidence stops in between. |
| P7.7 | Which S10 variant(s) go to the portfolio | 🔶 open |
| P5.3 evidence | May the nightly market-weight fit also learn from the S10 replay's history (asked 27 Sep, handoff §2)? | 🔶 open. **Recommendation (27 Sep, plan v2.3 P5.3 note): no.** The replay's dates overlap S10's design (`docs/S10_REPLAY_2026-09-26.md`: its form and width grid were chosen on a walk-forward whose test months include them), w = 0.6 was the best of a grid on that same sample, and its quotes may be up to 3 h old. The fit keeps learning from live shadow checkpoints only, which are the clean test. |
| P5.3 (amended) | Anchor the belief on the market | ✅ decided 27 Sep (in chat): Hassan approved the market-anchored belief. What a strategy believes starts from the book's own ladder, p = p_market + w (p_model - p_market), and the model earns weight w only on settled out-of-sample evidence (prior 0, bounds [0, 1]). (Until 30 Sep the scope's own gate tested its chosen w on the days that chose it; audit repair 4 made it walk forward.) Proposed after the engine replay of 12-25 Sep lost ~80% of the S11 and S12 ledgers trading the engine's probabilities against the market. |
| P3.6 cadence | When calibration may apply, and how often it updates | ✅ decided 28 Sep (in chat): Hassan: "the calibration model should kick in every 7 days, not 30, and at the 30 mark it runs a full recalibration based on the evidence collected." Built (#238): from 7 settlement dates the map updates every 7 days, weighted by dates / 30; at 30 dates a full recalibration at full weight; every update still has to beat the raw ladder on dates it never saw. |

## Merge rules (from the kickoff)

Claude merges a PR itself when CI is green and the acceptance check passes.
Hassan merges any PR that:
- changes grants, auth or security;
- deletes or rewrites existing rows;
- touches paper accounts or money settings;
- changes Actions schedules or budgets beyond what P6.1 specifies.

**In practice (session 1):** Claude merged #104 itself; the auto-mode guard
flagged it. Hassan then told Claude to merge #105–#110 in order, which Claude
did. **Hassan, 23 Sep, after #113: "yes, u can merge from here … keep that in
mind for later."** Claude merges its own PRs once CI is green. The categories
above still get Hassan's eye first: say so in the PR and ask.

Most are SQL views and functions that cannot read repository files. `edges.prob_id` holds a foreign key to `band_probabilities` (live), and the edges prune keeps the newest edge per band and side forever. **Found on the way, and fixed first: the prune corrupted the day-feature cache.** `prune_observations` cuts every city at one instant, part-way through most cities' local day, and the next `refresh_feature_cache` recomputed that day from what the cut left, overwriting the value cached while it was whole. Measured against the raw readings in `data/archive/observations`, on the nine cut dates since 18 Jun: of 470 cached city-days, 415 held fewer readings than the raw data and 152 a daily maximum too low (by 3.49 C on average, 12.0 C at worst, never too high); nyc 28 Jul was cached at 23.89 C from 2 readings against 26.67 C from 24. The first whole day after a cut also took its `prev_max_c`, delta and pressure change from the part-day. `derived_city_day_features` is what the weekly fit trains on. Fix (`20260929010000_a_cut_day_keeps_its_cached_values.sql`, the body in `sql/ad4_28` and `ad4_29`): a local day that began before the oldest reading held is never updated, and is inserted only if it was never cached (so the prune's guard can pass on a new database); the first whole day's day-over-day terms come from the cached day before. Proved live before applying: the view holds 3,189 city-days; the fix skips 51 cut days, one per city, all on 29-30 Jul, and writes every other day exactly as before. 47 of those 51 had already been overwritten by earlier refreshes, and 4 would have been overwritten at the next one. Contract `feature-cache-cut-day.cjs` (the real view and the real climb-profile view): a nyc evening and a Tokyo afternoon keep their cached values, the first whole day measures against the cached day, whole days match the view, and a rerun changes nothing. Checked to equal today's outputs on the days both cover, then run on the full history. 2. **One repository reader** for the Python long-history jobs: trajectory (120 d), regime (120 d), skill, hit tournament (120 d), station correction (75 d of model forecasts), predictive (45 d), backtest. (1) **Climb profile:** CRPS of the climb left on 259,715 city-day-hours, 1 Sep 2025 to 27 Sep 2026: 30 days 0.5359, 60 days 0.5444, 90 days 0.5568, all history 0.5767; 60 minus 30 +0.0085, 90% [+0.0072, +0.0098] (day-block bootstrap); 30 days is best in every quarter. (2) **Peak hour:** on 5,460 city-days, the recent 30 or 60 days of the same month give 1.187 h mean absolute error and 0.821 p10-p90 coverage, all history 1.182 h and 0.831: about equal. `derived_weather_peak` holds all 12 months for 53 cities and its refresh only upserts, so at a 30-day keep a month refreshes once 20 of its days are held and the existing row serves until then. Tests: `test_a_fit_trained_on_the_afternoon_it_predicts_is_never_promoted`; the evidence tests pass `advance_only=True`. **Part 2 design:** a nightly job appends the last days to the record (same windows, ~100 weighted Open-Meteo calls), fits the MOS with Rule 11 guards (prior = pooled fit, bound on the correction, a minimum per-city sample, a bounded nightly step, version on every row), writes forward rows for the open days from the current runs, and scores itself walk-forward; the engine then prices from the P3.9/MOS blend behind a switch, after the same checks P3.9 gets. **Part 2 (26 Sep).** `scripts/honest_record.py`: the record's daily rules in Python (Postgres half-up rounding, every window by 17:00); four real days of Amsterdam's raw answer reduce to the committed rows text for text (8 best_match, 56 model rows, `tests/fixtures/previous_runs_amsterdam_2025-07-18_4days.json`). `archive_observations.yml` appends the last 10 days nightly (2 requests a city) and commits `data/training` with the mirror. `scripts/station_mos.py` in `pipeline_daily` (6a3, after P3.9): per city and lead a ridge on the residual from the seven models' mean, pure Python by sufficient statistics (a fit 3.4 s, measured locally on the full record); the last station max it may see is lead + 1 days before the target, because it runs at ~05Z before the eve has ended (re-measured that way: lead 1 blend vs P3.9 +0.036 [0.031, 0.041], +1.1 pts [+0.5, +1.6], 11 of 11 months; lead 2 +0.027 [0.022, 0.031], +0.6 pts [+0.2, +1.1], 10 of 11); trains only on days before yesterday (yesterday has not ended west of about UTC-5 at 05Z). Rule 11: prior = pooled fit (its prior zero correction), bound |correction| <= 4 C, a city under 90 days uses the pool, a night moves a city's prediction on its own recent average day by at most 0.25 C (else that fraction of the step), version `station-mos:<as_of>:<hash>` on every row; each night scores itself on the last 30 days with a fit that ended before them against P3.9 replayed the same way (locally on 26 Aug-24 Sep, 1,440 city-days: models' mean 1.080, P3.9 0.836, model 0.825, blend 0.8125). Writes `derived_mos_coefficients` and `derived_mos_forecast` (migration `20260926150000`, applied live; service role only; mirrored; freshness spec rebuilt live in the guarded REPEATABLE READ transaction: 62 old rows identical both ways, owner/ACL/options/comments unchanged, 64 now, anon reads 64). Engine: `_blend_station_model` swaps P3.9's centre for the blend only where the model's row was built against the same P3.9 version, both versions in the reasons; all or nothing. Tests: `tests/test_the_honest_station_model.py` (16), `tests/database/station-model.cjs`. **Not yet run on a runner**: the sandbox cannot reach Open-Meteo (429) or the REST key; first real runs 27 Sep 02:36Z (append) and ~05Z (fit). **Wall-clock days (26 Sep, late).** Measured on the 48 saved answers: with `timezone=auto` Open-Meteo returns the whole range at ONE fixed offset, the city's offset at the moment of the request (every series exactly 439 x 24 h, no daylight-saving step anywhere; Wellington +13 for July). So a daylight-saving city's winter days sat an hour off, and a day re-fetched in another season would change. `honest_record` now asks `timezone=GMT`, reaches a day further each side, and groups hours by the city's IANA zone (`local_stamps`); the record was rebuilt from the same answers through that code: 6,336 of 42,144 best_match rows changed, all in daylight-saving cities, mostly Nov-Mar (and Wellington's winter); `tmax_00_17_c` moved 0.042 C on average, 5.3 C at most. Re-measured on the corrected record (lag as the nightly job sees it): lead 1 raw 1.324 / 23.4%, P3.9 0.934 / 33.7%, model 0.912 / 34.2%, blend 0.898 / 34.8%; blend vs P3.9 +0.036 [0.031, 0.041], +1.1 pts [+0.5, +1.7], 11 of 11 months; lead 2 +0.026 [0.021, 0.031], +0.5 pts [-0.0, +0.9], 11 of 11. Tests: wall-clock days in both seasons and hemispheres, the 25-hour autumn day, whole days only at the request's edges. **The same flaw is in `ingest_forecasts.py`** (P3.9's feed, `timezone=auto`); it re-fetches only its last 10 days, so its rows are right except around a daylight-saving change (Wellington's is 27 Sep; Europe 25 Oct; US 1 Nov); fixed next. **Labels whole days only (26 Sep, late):** `station_mos.load_labels` keeps a station maximum only once its local day has ended (see P3.9), both as the label and as the last maximum a forward row starts from; the step runs after "Derived recompute". **Re-measured on whole-day labels (26 Sep, late; the 43 partial rows removed):** lead 1 raw 1.324, P3.9 0.934, model 0.909, blend 0.898 / 34.75%; blend vs P3.9 +0.036 [0.032, 0.041], whole degree +1.0 pts [+0.4, +1.6]; lead 2 blend vs P3.9 +0.026 [0.021, 0.030]. The conclusion stands. **27 Sep check-in (06:30Z).** `P2.9_honest_record` (archive_observations 02:42Z) partial: 7,480 rows, 17-26 Sep, unreached amsterdam, moscow, lucknow, paris; main now holds best_match 42,234 rows and models 293,313 through 26 Sep (26 Sep: 45 and 47 cities). `P2.9_station_mos` (05:18Z) partial (unreached moscow, lucknow, paris, houston): 96 cells, 88 forward, 83 with P3.9, stepped 0, pooled_cities 0, version `station-mos:2026-09-27:8ea2d54103`; held out 27 Aug-25 Sep, lead 1 n 1,440: base 1.0756, P3.9 0.8367, model 0.8287, **blend 0.8137**; lead 2: 1.1793, 0.9518, 0.9600, **0.9407**. The fitter reads only that. Its map carries `evidence_scope frozen_day_ahead_v1`, and the engine applies only that scope (`CALIBRATION_EVIDENCE_SCOPE`), so the old hindsight map is refused even if marked applies. Rule 11: prior T=1, bounds [0.5, 2.0] (this module's priors), the existing gate as minimum sample, at most 25% change per night from the applied map (the plan's P5.8 guard), `calibration_version` on every band row, held-out dates only. The old market 'context' Brier (same late cutoff) is removed; the like-for-like market comparison is `v_checkpoint_scoreboard`. **What the numbers say:** on frozen forecasts the day-ahead ladder is calibrated below 0.5 and a temperature does not help out of sample. From 7 dates the map is updated every `UPDATE_EVERY_DAYS` (7) days (`schedule`): fitted on every complete ladder, moved from the T in force by the evidence weight `dates / 30` (`step_toward`, then the 25% step and the [0.5, 2.0] bounds), and applied only if it beats the raw ladder on the later dates it never saw (Brier gain >= 0.001 and a lower log loss, unchanged). When the evidence first reaches 30 dates, a full recalibration at full weight runs that night whatever the weekly clock says; the weekly updates after it are full-weight. On a night that is not due, `settings.calibration_map` is not written; the night's fit is logged as a diagnostic, with what is actually in force, so `v_calibration_status` and the Predictive page stay true (their wording updated). A same-night re-run repeats the night's update (P5.14). **Measured before shipping (28 Sep evidence, held-out dates 24-27 Sep, 179 ladders):** Brier 0.7726 raw, 0.7758 at T=1.15 (the first weekly update's weighted move), 0.7781 at 1.25, 0.7792 at 1.30; log loss 1.7111 raw, 1.7152, 1.7210, 1.7246. Every flattening makes the held-out days worse, so the first weekly update (29 Sep) should not apply: the day-ahead ladder lacks skill against the market (state block above), and temperature does not supply skill. Tests: `tests/test_calibration.py` (+7). **First weekly update (29 Sep 04:59Z, `calibration` log and `settings.calibration_map`):** update_kind weekly; 16 settlement dates, evidence_weight 0.5333 (16/30); T_fitted 1.2644, T = 1 + 0.5333 x 0.2644 = 1.1410, inside max_step 0.25 and bounds [0.5, 2.0]; updated_on 29 Sep, next_update_on 6 Oct; **applies false**: on the 5 held-out dates (226 ladders) Brier 0.7683 -> 0.7716 and log loss 1.6822 -> 1.6901, both worse. v_calibration_status as anon: state fitted_not_applied, validation_improves false. |

Measured against the live engine's price and the market's at 18:00 local the evening before (`d1_eve`).<br>**Lanes:** `asof` (issue time known) may one day promote; `research` (Open-Meteo previous-runs at nominal lead 1, issue time unverified) never does.<br>**Rule 11:** bounded parameters; max step per night (bias ±0.3 °C, width ±10%, weight ±0.10) against `prev_params`; `recipe_version` on every recipe row.<br>**State:** `shadow`. Previous-runs values reach the table on their own target day, after the cutoff, so without this no per-model forecast would ever count as known the evening before.<br>**Synthetic night:** 48 cities × 60 days × 3 models took 16.7 s a lane. Minutes held: `pipeline_daily` 30.0, `forecasts` 24.0; scheduled total 2,992 of 3,000.<br>Baseline: Baseline, `v_city_hit_history` 13–22 Sep day-ahead: top pick hit °C 31.3% (294 city-days) vs market 43.8% (283); °F 27.0% (89) vs market 47.1% (87). Settled city-days with a venue winner: 758, 26 Aug–22 Sep, 48 cities.<br>**First run, 24 Sep 09:37Z** (`derived_hit_summary`, d1_eve, forward): as-of lane, C 213 days: champion log loss 1.691 vs live 1.719 (gain vs live +0.030, 90% [+0.004, +0.080]) vs market 1.649 (gain vs market -0.003 [-0.305, +0.057]); hit rate champion 28.6%, live 33.2%, market 44.3%. As-of F 64 days: champion 1.641 vs live 1.908 (+0.282 [+0.159, +0.353]) vs market 1.473 (-0.162 [-0.291, -0.136]). The research lane (issue time unverified, never promotable) shows larger gains. Reading: a forward recipe beats the live engine in both units; nothing beats the market yet, and in F the market is ahead by a clear interval.<br>**24 Sep, Hassan's review of the city cards (San Francisco favoured 81 F at 2c while NWS said 73.4 F):** the engine prices from Open-Meteo alone. On 24 Sep for SF that model said 80-83 F against NWS's 73.4 F, and the engine's sigma of ~1 C put 0.1% on the 74-75 F bucket the market favoured. On 23 Sep Open-Meteo had been right (a floor of 78.8 F), so the flaw is overconfidence, not the one model. Measured on settled days (`v_city_hit_history`, 13-23 Sep, day-ahead, head to head): when the engine's favourite and the market's differed, the market's won C 62 of 155 and F 18 of 41, the engine's C 34 and F 8. On `v_verified_fact_band_outcome` (12-23 Sep, price 2-98c, gross per share): the side where the engine was above the market (YES) lost in every size band (-0.5 to -10.8 pp, 149 buckets); the side where it was below (NO) was positive in 4 of 5 bands (161 buckets). 159 `implausible_edge` anomalies in 24h across 30 cities are the same disagreement. Done now: the cards show the market's favourite beside the engine's, flag a dead favourite, forecasts that disagree by 1.5 C or more, and a centre outside every public forecast, and mark and sort after any trade against the market's favourite, quoting the live 30-day record. Next, in this step: the engine's width has to include the disagreement between forecasts, scored forward in the tournament before it goes live.<br>**The against-market gate, 24 Sep (all cities, `edge_engine`):** measured live across all 48 cities' soonest open ladders, the engine's favourite differed from the market's in 25, 6 favourites were under 5c, and 18 of 41 best trades backed the engine against the market's favourite. A trade backing the engine against the market's favourite (YES on a bucket the market does not favour, NO on the one it does, while the two favourites differ) is now `block_reason = against_market`. Rule 11: prior on; it lifts per unit only with 30 or more disagreement days in the last 30 and the lower 90% bound of (engine wins - market wins) per day above 0. It is recomputed each run from settled days, and its inputs are logged in the `edge_engine` ingest_log detail. Backtest on `v_verified_fact_band_outcome` (12-23 Sep, price 2-98c, disagreement of 3pp or more, gross per share): the blocked trades were 122 at -4.98 pp (se 2.64); the kept trades were 125 at -0.01 pp (se 2.55). The gate removes the losing half, and what is left breaks even.<br>**Width that grows with disagreement (24 Sep, shadow):** new tournament widths `1.0+spread` and `1.25+spread`, sigma = sqrt((w x rmse)^2 + spread^2), where spread is today's population SD across the lane's forecasts, capped at 3 C (Rule 11). They are offered where a city has 2 or more models. Tonight's `pipeline_daily` scores them forward in both lanes; the engine reads nothing until a recipe passes the gate (20 settled days per city; the as-of lane had 3-7 per city on 24 Sep). |

Caveats in the report: the dates overlap the P3.9 go-live replay; Previous Runs, not live runs; the P2.9 blend's own width not measured; by the v2.3 contract a width is promoted on dates after its design froze, which these are not - hence shadow first. Checked 27 Sep: P3.9 kept the width fitted to the raw forecast's errors (the go-live replay changed only the centre; centre MAE 1.124 -> 0.820 C). **Build part 1 (27 Sep evening).** `station_correction.py` fits the width from the same pairs each night. For each of the last `WIDTH_DAYS` (30) days it fits the combination on the 45 days before that day, and takes its error against the whole-day station max, per city and lead. Width = 1.2533 x (the city's summed absolute errors + 10 x pool MAE) / (n + 10); a city with fewer than `MIN_N` (5) days gets the pool. Rule 11: the pool is the prior; bounds are 0.5-3.0 C; a lead with fewer than 200 pooled errors gets no width; the width steps at most 10% a night from the width in force before that night (`prev_sigma_c`, P5.14); the version is `station-width:<as_of>:<hash>`. These are priors, and every run logs them. Rows go to `derived_station_width` (city, lead). Each open day's `derived_corrected_forecast` row carries `width_c` and `width_version`. A night whose width step fails keeps the correction, writes no widths and logs status `attention`. **Parity with the study:** on committed data as of 26 Sep, the module's widths equal the study's W2 formula for every city at leads 1 and 2 (max difference 0). Lead 1: pool 1.0426 C, MAE 0.8319, n 1,435. Lead 2: pool 1.1867 C. Committed data has no lead 3. At lead 1 cities range 0.640-1.578 C, so the bounds do not bind. The module adds `MIN_N` and the bounds to the study's formula; no city had fewer than 29 days. **Engine:** `probability_engine` records `band_probabilities.station_width_c` on every station-corrected price that has a stored width, whether or not it priced; `sigma_c` says what priced. It prices from the width only while the switch is on, at the forward row's lead <= `max_lead_days` (1). The label then joins `+station-width:<version>` to the station version, as the blend does, and the card adds "with the width fitted to their own errors". The width is fitted on P3.9's combination. Since 27 Sep 08:23Z the centre served is the blend with P2.9, and the blend's own width was not measured. The forward score is taken around the centre actually served, and that score decides. **Before the switch goes on, still to check:** ad4_45's calibration feedback reads `sigma_c`; how it treats a width fitted around another centre was not checked. **Part 2 (27 Sep evening): the forward score.** `scripts/station_width_score.py`, pipeline_daily step 7a3, runs after the station model. For each market the venue has confirmed since `FORWARD_FROM` (28 Sep, the first night the width was fitted) it takes the engine's last pricing before the city's local day, at lead >= 1, exactly as served. It compares that ladder with the same ladder re-priced with `station_width_c` around the same `centre_c`. The row proves that only the width differs: re-priced with its own `sigma_c`, the ladder must reproduce the stored `raw_prob` to 1e-4. Measured on all 134 markets confirmed for 24-26 Sep, every ladder reproduced (median 4e-6, largest 3.6e-5). A market is skipped and named when: there is no stored width; the width priced (the switch was on); there is a floor or a calibration map; the ladder is incomplete; or the winner is not on the ladder. The scores are log loss (floor 1e-6), Brier, top pick (ties by band id), and CRPS and 80% coverage where a whole-day station maximum exists. Each market gets one immutable row in `fact_station_width_score`. The verdict, logged as `P3.9_width_score`, is the mean log-loss gain per city-day with a 90% date-block bootstrap interval (2,000 resamples, seed 11). It reads "ready" once there are 7 or more dates and the lower bound is above zero. The script turns nothing on. A market is scorable once its last day-ahead pricing came after the first fit (28 Sep ~05:15Z) and the venue has confirmed it. At about 19:00Z on 27 Sep the newest confirmed date was 26 Sep. The first verdict with 7 dates therefore comes roughly a week after the first scorable date; not measured yet. **Check-in 28 Sep 05:45Z:** the first nightly width wrote `station-width:2026-09-28:6a6b2c7117` (no error): 144 rows (48 cities x 3 leads), sigma 0.63-1.64 at lead 1, 0.74-1.93 at lead 2, 0.82-2.34 at lead 3, all equal to the fitted sigma (first night, prev null); the pooled lead-1 width is 1.0194 C over 1,422 pairs (the study: 1.04 C). Every one of tonight's 229 corrected-forecast rows carries its city's width at the lead clamped to 1-3 (lead 0 uses lead 1, `width_for`). No pricing has run since the 05:02Z fit (the next is 08:36Z), so `band_probabilities.station_width_c` is not checked yet. **v_data_freshness rebuilt live** from sql/ad4_39_freshness.sql in one REPEATABLE READ transaction that raised on any difference: the 64 old rows identical by EXCEPT ALL both ways, exactly two new rows (derived_station_width ok 144, fact_station_width_score empty), v_data_health and v_provisional_settings unchanged but for the count, grants and options unchanged; both read as anon (66 rows; 2 empty: backtest_trades as before, and the width score). The width score logged ok with 0 scored: it scores markets from 28 Sep on, and none is confirmed yet. |

Live 24 Sep 18:20Z: `fact_checkpoint_outcome` holds 0 rows and `prediction_checkpoints` 20 (target dates 24 and 25 Sep, none settled), so today's fit is the prior for every bin. | `scripts/belief.py`: a Beta-binomial reliability map per 0.05 bin of `p_model`, half-open like the venue's buckets (0.60 is in [0.60, 0.65); float noise had put it in the bin below, and a test pins it). Partial pooling runs pooled -> city cluster (when P5.9 supplies clusters) -> checkpoint class. The six P4.2 checkpoints fold into the plan's five classes (d1_eve pre_day, morning, noon midday, prepeak_2h and prepeak_1h pre_peak, postpeak_1h post_peak). Each level is a prior of strength K0 = 30 centred on the level above; at the pooled level that centre is `p_model`. `ladder_posterior` renormalises the means to 1 and scales each sd with its mean. Learns from `prediction_checkpoints.probs` joined to `fact_checkpoint_outcome.winner_band_id`, target dates strictly before `as_of`, and only from ladders that hold the venue winner. Rule 11: prior K0 = 30 and max step 25% are the plan's numbers. The minimum sample N_MIN = 20 per scope-bin and the bounds [0.001, 0.999] are this module's priors, not measurements, and are written into every fitted table. The step is held by moving a bin's wins, never its n. Versions are deterministic (`belief:<as_of>:<sha>`) and returned with every posterior. `load()` reads `strategy_params` (param `belief`, created by P5.8) and falls back to the prior while that table does not exist. Nothing reads beliefs yet; the P5.5 solver will. Rule 11: prior 0 (the market), bounds [0, 1], a scope (view source x checkpoint class: `engine:pre_day`, `s10:post_peak`, ...) moves only with >= 20 settled days (the replay contract's floor) and a day-block bootstrap lower 90% bound of the log-loss gain over w = 0 above zero, at most 0.05 a night (this module's prior), version on every decision (`versions.market_anchor`); fit() reads only dates before `as_of`; `load()` returns the prior while `strategy_learning` is off. Applied in `strategies/engine_views.engine_input`, the one door the tick and the replay both use, for every model view (S10, S11, S12; S2 is prices only). **Measured before building it** (the replay's own inputs, 12-25 Sep: 3,019 checkpoints on 13 days with a complete engine ladder and a fully priced book, scored on the venue winner): log loss 1.006 at w = 0 against 1.847 at w = 1; every w on a 0.1 grid above 0 was worse than 0 in every group - day-ahead (d1_eve) n 491 1.225 vs 1.835, same day n 2,528 0.964 vs 1.849, C n 2,280 0.985 vs 1.882, F n 739 1.072 vs 1.740. So at the prior, S11 and S12 do not trade (on the 10 real 26 Sep ladders tested: no-trade band or nothing tradeable, every time) until a model earns weight. **Also ported into the engine:** the platform's against-market gate (`edge_engine.against_market_rows`, Hassan 24 Sep "never favour losing bets"): while it is on - its default, and live on for both units - no YES on a bucket the market does not favour and no NO on its favourite when the view's favourite differs from the market's; a lock is exempt (it cannot lose). `decision_engine.against_market_assets`, reason `against_market`. **Part 3, the nightly fit (27 Sep; plan/p5-3-market-weight-fit).** Until now nothing fitted the weight, so every scope would stay at 0 forever. `strategy_learn.py` now fits `market_anchor.fit` each night into `strategy_params` (param `market_weight`, one immutable row, written only when the evidence changed). The rows (`market_rows`): each engine checkpoint (`prediction_checkpoints.probs`) against the book the tick stored beside it (`.market`, read by `market_anchor.market_probs`, the function the engine uses at a decision), scored on the venue winner (`fact_checkpoint_outcome`), scope `engine:<class>`. S10's remaining-day ladder for the same city, date and checkpoint (`s10_shadow_checkpoints`) is scored against that same book and winner, scope `s10:<class>`. A checkpoint with an unquoted bucket, or a ladder without the winner, is left out; target dates are strictly before the night it runs. Live on 27 Sep: 395 engine checkpoints were settled, over at most 4 target dates per class (24-27 Sep). So every scope is held at the prior until it has 20 settled days. S10 has no rows yet: its shadow rows were not being written (P7.4, fixed in #216). Tests: `tests/test_strategy_learn.py` (+2). **Noticed, not changed:** the platform has two definitions of the market's price for a one-sided book. The tick's `market_price` (and `public.book_mark()`) uses the last trade clamped to the quoted side; `market_anchor.market_probs` uses half the ask, or halfway from the bid to 1. The fit and the engine both use the second, so they agree with each other. **S10's weight, measured on the P7.3 replay (27 Sep; plan/s10-market-weight):** `docs/S10_MARKET_WEIGHT_2026-09-27.md`, `tools/s10_market_weight.py`. S10's walk-forward ladders from the replay (its model fitted only on days before each target's week) were scored against the market the engine would have read at each checkpoint: every band's newest top of book within 3 h, via `market_anchor.market_probs` (`data/replay/inputs_s10_market_2026-09-27`, README there). Of 7,341 checkpoints, 3,001 had a complete market ladder, on 21 days between 27 Aug and 25 Sep. Log loss at w = 0 (the market) against the best w: post_peak 0.620 vs 0.559 at w = 0.6 (591 rows, 18 days); midday 1.049 vs 1.040 at 0.2 (609, 21); pre_peak 1.007 vs 1.005 at 0.1 (1,207, 21); morning 1.139, best at 0 (594, 16). Day-block bootstrap of the gain per row at w = 0.6: post_peak +0.061, lower 90% +0.031 (18 days); midday -0.031 (lower -0.067); pre_peak -0.057 (lower -0.082). So S10 beats the market after the peak and not before it. `market_anchor.fit`, walk-forward weekly, moves no scope: post_peak has 18 days, under MIN_DAYS 20, and midday and pre_peak have no positive lower bound. **Not changed:** the nightly fit learns only from live shadow checkpoints. Using the replay's evidence in it is a decision for Hassan (it would let post_peak move once it has 20 days). Even then, each night moves at most 0.05, so reaching 0.6 takes 12 nights. |
| **P5.4** | Execution cost model | **done** (#161) | #161, plan/p5-4-execution-cost | fixture tests on two real YES books from `book_snapshots` (snapshots 293949 and 292972, 24 Sep): buying exactly the shares within 5c of the touch spends exactly the `ask_usd_5c` the pipeline stored beside each book (20.3684 and 12.66). | `scripts/execution_cost.py`. **Taker:** `taker_curve` (one segment per ask level: cost per share = price + the venue fee rate*p*(1-p), rising with quantity) and `taker_cost` (price, fee, total, average, marginal and limit, and `fully_filled` false when the ladder is too thin). It agrees with `paper_plans.ladder_cost` (the order path) and `cost_model.walk_ladder` on the same book. **Exit:** `exit_proceeds` walks the bids dearest first and deducts the fee. **Maker:** the plan's priors. P_fill is 0.2 at the touch for 1 h as a constant hazard (1 - 0.8^t), stopping at the close. Adverse selection is 0.5 x spread; makers pay no fee. A bid below the touch gets P_fill 0 under the prior (no evidence yet of how often it fills, and 0 never invents an edge). A bid at or above the best ask is not a maker order, so it returns None. Rule 11: the bounds [0.01, 0.9] for P_fill at the touch and [0, 1] for the adverse fraction are this module's priors. `load()` reads learned values from `strategy_params` (P5.8), clips them and records their versions, and falls back to the priors while the table does not exist. `band_liquidity` is accepted for the learned model and unused by the prior. | **P5.5** | Holdings solver | **part 1 done** (#162); the objective decided and built (#164); **part 2 merged** (#166; the "PR open" here was stale until 27 Sep) | #162, plan/p5-5-solver-part1, plan/p5-5-robust-objective | the plan's tests: the closed form equals the numeric optimum on 12 random ladders (every fraction within 2e-4, growth within 1e-7); no trade when every bucket costs at least its probability; a book passing the lock never loses; S10 picks the growth bucket, not the likely one. | `scripts/holdings_solver.py`, pure Python. Adding scipy to `requirements.runtime.txt` would install it on every scheduled workflow (Rule 7), and it is not needed for this part. `horse_race()` is Kelly's closed form (YES only, fee-inclusive, no caps). `solve()` is Cover's multiplicative update for the log-optimal portfolio over cash, YES and NO assets, restricted to the strategy's allowed sides; it is monotone to the optimum for any payoff matrix. Also: `fractional()` (lambda: prior 0.25, bounds [0.05, 0.5]), `worth_trading()` (h: prior 0.002, bounds [0.0005, 0.01]; fees are already in the prices, so they are not subtracted twice), `lock_holds()`, and `best_single_bucket()` (S10). A 20% bucket at 10c beats a 50% bucket at 45c: growth pays for edge relative to price. **Finding, and the decision:** the plan's objective, expected log-growth averaged over posterior draws, equals the growth at the mean ladder exactly (mean_d sum_k pi_dk log W_k = sum_k mean(pi_k) log W_k). The draws cancel, so p_sd could not shrink a bet as the plan intends; `test_the_draw_average_ignores_p_sd` proves it. Hassan, 24 Sep: "do the effective, efficient, smart and logical way". So `solve_robust()` maximises mean growth over the worst ALPHA of 200 seeded Dirichlet draws (a CVaR, still concave). It is solved by mirror ascent: a Cover step on the worst-draws ladder, with the running mean of the second half of the iterates. ALPHA: prior 0.25, bounds [0.05, 1.0]; this module's prior, not a measurement, to be learned in P5.8. ALPHA = 1 is the plan's mean objective. Measured on a two-edge ladder (p 0.5/0.3/0.2 at 40c/20c/45c): the mean book stakes 47.4% of the ledger; the robust book stakes 46.0% at sd 0.005, 38.9% at 0.03, 24.1% at 0.08 and 3.7% at 0.15, in about 0.4 s a ladder. Six new tests: stake shrinks as sd grows; a sure belief is staked like the mean book (within 5%); ALPHA 1 equals solve() on the sample-mean ladder; never worse than the mean book on the worst draws; no edge, no trade; ALPHA bounded and the answer reproducible by seed. **Part 2:** caps (depth, risk layer, rails) as upper bounds, existing holdings and the bid ladder for sells, the lock inside the optimiser, the output record of binding constraints. **Part 2 (24 Sep):** `solve_book()` buys the growth-optimal NEW positions under caps (depth, the risk layer and the rails, as a maximum share of wealth per asset), around what the ledger already holds, and with the lock. Objective: the mean ladder, or the worst ALPHA of draws when sds are given. Method: Cover steps projected onto the capped simplex (the KL projection: cap, rescale the free coordinates, repeat), averaged over the second half of the iterates. The lock: the book, holdings plus purchases, may not end below the ledger's wealth in any outcome. When the growth-optimal book breaks it, the best mix on the segment from the equal-shares insurance book (all YES, or all NO) to it is taken; with no equal-shares book costing less than it surely pays, nothing is bought. Found while testing: on a ladder costing under $1 a set, Kelly bets by p rather than equal shares and can still lose (W = 0.64 if the 20% bucket wins), so the first version's plain veto refused a sure profit. Binding caps and the lock are returned in `binding`, for the decision log (P5.11). Selling what is held is left to the no-trade band and timing (P5.6), against the bid ladder. Tests: 13 new (51 in the file): no constraints = the exact optimum; a binding cap holds and beats a brute-force grid; a loose cap changes nothing; a held position is not bought twice (holdings plus purchases = the unconstrained optimum); the lock never loses on 6 random ladders and takes the sure profit on an under-$1 ladder; robust with caps; the projection keeps free ratios. |
| **P5.6** | Timing: act now or wait | **part 1 merged** (#174; the "PR open" here was stale until 27 Sep): the rule and the prior; wired into the tick at P5.12 | plan/p5-6-timing | the tests below; no live effect until P5.12 calls it. | `scripts/timing.py`: act if G_now >= E[max(G_next, 0)] - c_wait, G_next simulated from a transition model of observed one-hour changes of (p_post, best ask, depth) per cell (hours to peak, model-market gap, regime, liquidity). Pairs are consecutive observations 50-70 min apart. Below 500 pairs in a cell (the plan's number) the prior rule decides - act when G_now > 0 and the gap is not shrinking - and the record says so. c_wait prior 0, bounded [0, 0.01]; the cell edges are this module's priors and part of TIMING_VERSION on every decision. Fitting the model is P5.8. Tests: `tests/test_timing.py` (14). |

| **P5.9** | Risk layer | **part 1 (the fixed rails) done** (#168, applied live) plus the plan builder sized inside them (#169); **part 2 built** (plan/p5-9-clusters): clusters, correlated exposure and drawdown scaling in the engine, and the weekly fit in the nightly loop; **part 3 built** (#237): the 8% cluster-day rail in `queue_plan`, and a plan's legs counted once | #168, plan/p5-9-rails, plan/p5-9-rails-sizing, plan/p5-9-clusters | after applying live: settings `risk_rails` holds the defaults and `trading_halt` is off, and the live `queue_plan` carries the rail checks (md5 recorded). | `20260924110000_fixed_risk_rails.sql`: settings `risk_rails` (daily_loss_frac 0.05, city_day_frac 0.03, cluster_day_frac 0.08, max_price 0.97, close_buffer_min 15, decided_by) and `trading_halt` (the kill switch; `set_trading_halt(halted, reason)`, service_role only, and a halt needs a reason). `queue_plan` (the live body, md5 737994a7) gains the checks every automatic or approved entry passes, whoever proposed it: the halt; today's realised loss against 5% of the day's starting equity; the price bound per leg; no entry within 15 min of the market's close; and held plus reserved plus this plan on one city-day at most 3% of equity (at cost). Measured: `markets` has no scheduled close, so the close is the end of the market's local day (`cities.timezone`). Exits are not railed, so a desk can always get out. Clusters are part 2; until then a city is its own cluster, so the 3% rail binds and the 8% one cannot. `scripts/risk_rails.py` reads the same settings row (DEFAULTS only as a fallback; a test holds them equal to the migration's seed). `holdings_solver.solve_book` takes `max_spend` (the ladder's city-day budget, as a floor on cash, via an exact bisection KL projection) and `max_price`. So the solver plans inside the rails instead of being refused. Found while testing: under a tight budget the optimum is NOT Kelly scaled down; the spend goes first to the best edge per dollar, and a grid over every split agrees. Tests: `tests/test_risk_rails.py` (8), plus a contract block 'THE FIXED RAILS'. The harness asserts the migration seeded exactly the defaults, then relaxes them for the older contracts, whose $100-$300 test desks buy far above 3% to test other things; the rails block restores and tests them. **Sizing inside the rails:** `paper_plans.prepare` gets `rail_room()`, the same arithmetic queue_plan enforces (3% of equity at cost, less what the city-day already holds and reserves). A plan is sized down to fit instead of refused, since the shadow ledgers are $1,000 and 3% is $30 a city-day. A plan over the price bound, or while halted, is refused before any read, with the rail named. 3 tests. **Part 2 (27 Sep).** **The measurement first.** The plan names `derived_city_correlation`, but that table is not a sound input. It correlates `weather_forecasts` against UTC-day observation maxima, and it counts every forecast row as a day. On 27 Sep its newest fit had `n_days` from 299 to 2,559, while the rows `recompute_correlation` reads by default (lead 1, last 180 days) cover 62 dates, with up to 14 rows per city-day. It had 360 of 1,326 pairs above 0.5. The fit here uses the honest record instead (P2.9): the day-ahead best_match maximum minus the station's whole local day (`derived_city_day_features.max_c`), for 48 cities. On the raw errors the top pairs were jeddah-shenzhen 0.41 and amsterdam-helsinki 0.36; a pair like jeddah-shenzhen is a shared drift, not shared weather. After removing each city's own mean error over its previous 30 days (the pricing engine removes a trailing station bias, P3.9), 386 complete dates remained. The top pairs became austin-houston 0.32, qingdao-zhengzhou 0.31 and amsterdam-london 0.28; 2 pairs were above 0.3 and none above 0.5. Ledoit-Wolf shrinkage toward constant correlation (intensity 0.56) brought the largest pair down to 0.15. Fitted on the first half of the dates and scored against the second half's sample correlation, the RMSE was 0.117 for the sample matrix, 0.094 shrunk, and 0.096 for the identity. So day-ahead forecast errors are nearly independent across these cities, and the shrunk estimate is no worse than assuming independence. **Built.** `scripts/city_clusters.py`: `residuals`, `detrended` (reads only the past), `matrix` (cities with at least 90 complete dates in the last 365), `ledoit_wolf` (pure Python, 0.1 s on 360 x 48), `clusters` (connected components at a shrunk rho of 0.5 or more), `fit`, `due` (weekly), and `room`. `room` holds a city-day's new spend to two limits: the 8% cluster rail over the cluster's exposure on that date, and 8% over own exposure plus rho-weighted exposure on every other city that date. So the cap shrinks as correlation rises: at rho 1 it is the cluster rail, and at rho 0 only the 3% city-day rail binds. `risk_rails.drawdown_scale` implements the plan's rule, max(0.25, 1 - drawdown/0.20) from the ledger's high-water mark. `decision_engine.decide` takes the tightest of the city-day, cluster and correlated rooms (`reason_code` `cluster_day_full` or `correlated_full`). It applies the drawdown scale after lambda's own bounds, so a learned lambda at its floor is still cut, and records the clusters version on every decision. The ledger gains optional `same_day` and `high_water_usd`; without them the engine decides exactly as before. The engine replay (P5.12 part 2) now passes both: at cost, the strategy's holdings on the other cities of the same date, and the peak of its equity at each settlement (`tests/test_replay_engine.py`, 1 more). **Rule 11.** A pair's rho has a prior of 0.30 (`risk_budget.DEFAULT_CORRELATION`: "a missing pair is not an uncorrelated pair"), bounds [0, 1], a minimum of 90 complete dates per city, a step of at most 0.10 per refit from the previous version (the first from the prior), and a version. The fit reads dates strictly before `as_of`. Because each refit moves at most 0.10 from the 0.30 prior, the first weekly fits sit well above the measured values (first fit: 0.20 or 0.40). While `settings.strategy_learning` is off, `city_clusters.load` returns the prior, so every pair counts at 0.30. The arithmetic: with 3% already on each of k other city-days that date, the correlated room is 8% - 0.3 x 3% x k, which reaches 0 at k = 9 with no own exposure. **Nightly.** `strategy_learn.py` refits when the last `city_clusters` row is 7 days old, from the record in the repository and the whole-day labels, and writes one immutable row. No new workflow or schedule is added. Tests: `tests/test_city_clusters.py` (14), which include the plan's two: the cluster cap binds on correlated fixtures, and a full cluster blocks the order whatever the solver asks. `tests/test_strategy_learn.py` adds 2 (weekly and versioned; nothing written without a measured city). **Not done:** `queue_plan` does not yet enforce the cluster rail in the database (part 3; with every measured cluster a single city it would not bind today). The belief layer's cluster pooling (P5.3) is not fed clusters. The first nightly cluster row is to be verified on the next `pipeline_daily` run. **Part 3 (28 Sep).** Migration `20260928090000_the_cluster_rail.sql` replaces `queue_plan` (the live body's md5 matched the 24 Sep file's, 95f70d71). Every automatic or approved entry now also holds the cost held, the BUY orders reserved and the plan's legs, over every city of a leg's cluster on the same date, to `risk_rails.cluster_day_frac` (0.08) of equity. Membership is the latest `city_clusters` fit, read only while `settings.strategy_learning` is on, as `city_clusters.load` reads it for the engine, so the engine plans inside the rail it is checked against. It is off live (28 Sep), so every city is its own cluster and the 3% rail binds first. The first nightly fit (28 Sep) also put every city in its own cluster. The correlated (rho-weighted) room stays in the engine: a sizing rule, not one of the plan's fixed rails. **Found and fixed on the way:** a plan's legs were counted twice. `queue_plan` inserts each leg's order before it checks the next leg, and the city-day sum counted those orders as well as every leg of the plan. The contract reproduced it on the old body: three $9 legs on one city-day, $27 of a $30 room, refused at the second leg as "Rail: 36.00 on london ... would exceed 0.03". An S11 ladder is such a plan. The step-2 engine contract used a one-leg plan, which is why nothing caught it. The sums now leave out the plan's own orders. Contract (THE FIXED RAILS block): the ladder queues; $31 on the city-day is still refused and $29 passes; three cities at $27 all queue with learning off; with a fitted table naming them one cluster, the third is refused as "Rail: 81.00 on the berlin cluster ... would exceed 0.08"; on another date they pass. |

Log-growth per dollar is log(E'/E) / (stake/E) per settled decision, in closing order: the return per dollar for a small stake, and finite on a total loss. Allocation: a Normal-inverse-gamma posterior per regime (`regime_label` on the trade; prior mean 0, kappa 10, alpha 2, beta 1), with regimes under 20 decisions falling back to the strategy's pooled posterior, mixed by the strategy's regime frequencies; one Thompson draw each; weights proportional to max(draw, 0) water-filled under the cap. Rule 11: the cap is hard-bounded at 0.40 whatever the setting says; a weight moves at most 0.10 a night (a strategy that has left the portfolio state drops to 0 at once); nothing is allocated before the gate's sample; every allocation is versioned `thompson-v1:<date>` and seeded by that version, so it can be reproduced; fitted on shadow ledgers and tested by the portfolio's later fills. Order sizing: `signal_engine` stamps each signal with `sized_on_usd`, the ledger bankroll its size was computed on. `paper_plans.allocation_room` gives a portfolio plan at most weight x equity less the strategy's open trades and live BUY reservations on the account, and scales the engine size by allocated capital / `sized_on_usd`. A signal without that base is refused rather than guessed at. The allocation record goes into the plan's evidence. `strategy_lifecycle` now judges portfolio-state strategies on their shadow ledgers too (portfolio -> suspended on the same bound).
