None was BUY, SELL or SWITCH, so the replay is proven on the no-trade path only; the order path stays unproven until the engine trades. | `scripts/decision_engine.py`, `decide(view, book=, ledger=, rails=, halted=, params=, state=, hours_to_close=)`: view (a strategy's probabilities and constraints: `allow`, `only`, `lock`, `sds`) -> `belief.ladder_posterior` -> `holdings_solver.solve_book` (robust on the posterior sds, the strategy's caps, the lock, the 0.97 price rail) -> `fractional` (lambda) -> the city-day rail (`risk_rails.ladder_budget`) -> the no-trade band (`worth_trading`, h) -> `timing.decide` -> IOC legs in `leg_order`. Halted, daily-loss and a full city-day return before any solve; every decision carries the versions used (engine, belief, lambda, h, timing) and each binding constraint. Pure: every input is an argument, so the tick and the replay can differ only by what they pass. **Found building it:** capping at the city-day rail and then multiplying by lambda shrinks a bet twice (a 50% bucket at 35c grew a $1,000 ledger by 0.0012, under h's 0.002, so nothing traded); the plan's order is the Kelly book times lambda, then the caps, and that is what this does (scaling toward cash keeps a lock a lock). **Measured with the plan's priors** (belief prior sd, worst quarter of 200 draws, lambda 0.25, h 0.002, 3% city-day), one bucket alone on a $1,000 ledger trades only at a wide edge: p 0.5 at an ask <= 0.30 (not 0.35), p 0.7 <= 0.50 (not 0.55), p 0.3 <= 0.15 (not 0.20). A multi-bucket horse race clears it more easily (4-bucket test ladder: $30, the whole rail). Whether that bar is right is for the replay (part 2) and P5.8 to show on settled days, not for a prior to be moved here. **Part 1 limits:** orders are IOC takers (the maker option needs the fill simulator in the loop: part 2); selling is the strategies' own rules (S10's certainly-lost and switch), as `solve_book` says. **Part 2:** the replay harness calls this with as-of inputs (raw_book ladders from 12 Sep, trade prints for maker fills) for every shadow strategy's view. **Measured 27 Sep (P8.2 part 1):** one decision costs 0.52-0.78 s on average (max 1.13 s) in pure Python on the real 26 Sep ladders - 4,000 solver iterations over 200 posterior draws. The tick has one billed minute; every open market times seven engine strategies is ~665 decisions, ~400 s. Part 3 needs a faster solver or decisions only for city-days whose inputs moved (the plan's due rules, P7.4), measured before it is wired. **Speed work, 27 Sep (plan/p5-12-solver-speed):** (1) `solve_book`'s inner loop rewritten to add the same terms in the same order without per-element indexing - bit-identical (a golden file of the previous code's outputs on 30 cases, `tests/test_holdings_solver_speed.py`), 1.4x faster (72 random cases 9.72 s -> 6.88 s). (2) Fewer iterations is NOT safe: on the 20 real ladders 1,000 and 4,000 iterations gave the same 73 decisions (max leg difference $0.01), but on 300 random ladders 1 action differed and 8 legs moved by more than $0.50 (max $13.97); so the iteration count stays. (3) A numpy version of the same loop (a prototype, not committed) gave identical decisions on the same 300 (max leg difference $0.0000, growth within 2e-16) at 0.11 s a decision against 0.31 s pure - only 2.7x, the projections still being Python. The worst hour measured so far had 25 city-days due (P4.2), ~150 engine decisions: ~46 s pure, ~17 s with numpy, inside a tick whose job already runs close to its one billed minute, while Actions minutes are over budget (measured ~4,790 a month against 3,000). How the engine runs hourly is therefore a design and minutes decision for part 3 (numpy in the tick's runtime, a faster projection, or fewer due decisions), to be measured and put to Hassan, not squeezed in. **Part 3:** the tick calls it for due city-days, orders go through `queue_plan` into the shadow ledgers, `decisions` rows carry `g_now`/`g_wait`/`binding`, `SWITCH` joins the action check, and SELL/SWITCH get a consumer; the tick's 60 s is measured before and after. **Part 2, the replay (27 Sep).** `scripts/backtest/replay_engine.py` runs S11 (`s11_ladder`, `s11_lock`), S12 and S2 through `decision_engine.decide` via `engine_views.engine_input`, each on its own $1,000 ledger, at the tick's checkpoints over 12-25 Sep: 648 venue-confirmed city-days, 15,336 decisions. Inputs are as of each decision: the engine's own ladder (`band_probabilities`, no older than 6 h) and the book (`book_snapshots` with depth tiers, no older than 3 h). Fills walk the depth tiers at each tier's worse edge plus the taker fee; NO legs draw on the YES bids. Settlement is at the local day's end on the venue's winner. The daily-loss rail is fed realised P&L per UTC day. The inputs are in `data/replay/inputs_engine_2026-09-27/` (README there): the books were trimmed from 287,011 to 35,515 rows, keeping every row a decision can read. A rerun on the trimmed books gave byte-identical decision rows and identical numbers. Report: `docs/ENGINE_REPLAY_2026-09-27.md`; rows: `data/replay/engine_replay_2026-09-27.csv.gz`. Tests: `tests/test_replay_engine.py` (8). **Result:** `s11_ladder` filled $2,488 for -$804.67, ending at $195.33 (max drawdown 0.816). `s12_no` filled $6,633 for -$803.66, ending at $196.34. `s11_lock` made +$4.21 on 3 trades; `s2` made +$16.07 on 1. Per filled leg, S11 bought where the view said 0.281 against an ask of 0.159, and the bucket won 0.142 of the time. S12 bought NO at a view of 0.828 against an ask of 0.647; it won 0.623 of the time. The engine's probabilities are further from the truth than the market exactly where they disagree with it. That led to the market-anchored belief (P5.3 amended, #213). **Also found:** `s12_no` made 1,078 trades on 521 markets over 14 days. Nothing capped its exposure across the city-days of one date, because that cap is P5.9 part 2. `s11_lock` lost $1.47 on one book where tier slippage broke the lock. **Not replayed:** S10 (its remaining-day view needs the P7.3 inputs as of each decision); maker fills; the timing model (the prior rule decided every WAIT). **Not yet measured:** the acceptance match against live `decisions` rows, which needs part 3. **Lock floor fixed (27 Sep; plan/p5-12-lock-floor).** The lock required the ladder's wealth to stay at least 1 in every outcome, but that wealth is only the free cash plus this ladder's holdings; money on other ladders is not in its outcomes. So while the ledger held anything elsewhere, every lock "broke". In the anchored replay all 119 `lock_breaks` of `s11_lock` fell between 10:25Z and 21:00Z on 19 Sep, the life of its one position (tel_aviv, bought 10:20Z, settled at local midnight). The floor is now (cash + this ladder's holdings at cost) / equity: `holdings_solver.solve_book(lock_floor=)`, `_lock_book(floor=)` and decide's own check. It is 1 when nothing is held elsewhere, so every earlier result is unchanged there (the solver's golden file passes). Test: `test_a_lock_is_still_a_lock_while_other_ladders_hold_money` fails on the old code (`lock_breaks`) and passes now. **The anchored replay (27 Sep, main at ce812ea: #213 anchor, #215 rooms, #218 lock floor):** `docs/ENGINE_REPLAY_ANCHORED_2026-09-27.md`, with its rows in `data/replay/engine_replay_anchored_2026-09-27.csv.gz`. Same 648 city-days and 15,336 decisions. `s11_ladder` 1 trade, +$22.42; `s11_lock` 1, +$16.52; `s12_no` 2, -$1.37; `s2` 1, +$16.07. The three winners are the same book, tel_aviv 19 Sep post-peak, whose YES asks summed below 1. Why the rest did not trade, per strategy of 3,834: S11: no-trade band 2,963, no market to anchor on (a bucket unquoted) 168, nothing tradeable 57, against the market 18. Lock: no-trade band 3,038 and no market 168; after #218 there are no `lock_breaks` (119 before). S12: no-trade band 2,984, no market 168, nothing tradeable 35, against the market 18. All four: no pricing within 6 h 627. P5.9's rooms never bound, because nothing else was held on the same date. The per-leg table now shows the model's probability and the probability the engine decided on; the first report's "view's probability" was the model's. **The solve is skipped when nothing can pass the band (P5.12 part 3 prep).** `holdings_solver.no_book_grows`: the mean growth the no-trade band compares is concave in the weights. If moving cash into every buyable asset lowers it at the current book, no book the solver could return passes the band, so `decide` returns NONE / `no_trade_band` without solving. A lock qualifies only while it holds nothing on its ladder. Proved two ways: `tests/test_zero_edge_shortcut.py` (32 random ladders, some with holdings, locks and money elsewhere: action, reason and orders identical with and without it), and the full anchored replay rerun with it. All 15,336 decisions were identical (action, reason, fills, amounts) and the run took 225 s against 1,328 s, 5.9x. The shortcut run overlapped another replay for about 2 min, so 225 s is if anything high. In-process, S12 on 19 Sep: 234 of 294 solves skipped, 189.9 s -> 39.1 s. **Part 3a, the engine decides in the tick, recorded only (27 Sep; plan/p5-12-part3a).** `scripts/engine_shadow.py`, called from `tick.run` after S10's shadow step, within the tick's own budget; it never raises. For each city-day checkpoint the tick wrote (the latest per city-day), each engine strategy decides through `engine_views.engine_input` and `decision_engine.decide`, the code the replay runs: S10's three variants on the remaining-day ladder `s10_shadow` computed in the same tick, and `s11_ladder`, `s11_lock` and `s12_no` on the checkpoint's own ladder. The book is the tick's CLOB top (a NO leg's ask is 1 - the YES bid). The ledger is the strategy's own shadow ledger, read as `queue_plan` reads it: equity at cost, the city-day's positions plus live BUY reservations, same-date exposure for P5.9's rooms, and the realised high-water mark. The learned tables come through their loaders (the priors while `strategy_learning` is off). One `decisions` row per strategy and city-day: the action, a reason code (engine_views' reasons in words are mapped: `no_market_anchor`, `no_ladder`, `no_ledger`, `own_rule_<action>`), g_now/g_wait, binding, target and held dollars, `n_signals` = legs, and every version used (`params_version`). A strategy with no view is a row too. **Nothing is ordered.** `20260927150000_engine_strategies_are_registered.sql` registers the six strategies disabled, in research: signal_engine and paper_plans act only on enabled ones, and the lifecycle trigger opens each its $1,000 shadow ledger. The same migration adds SWITCH to `decisions_action_check`. `s2_combination_arb` stays on the old signal path until that path is retired, so no id is decided by two engines. Tests: `tests/test_engine_shadow.py` (8), plus a contract block (the six are registered, disabled, in research, one ledger each; a re-run changes nothing; it fails if one is registered enabled). **Measured on 39 real checkpoints of the last 4 h (27 Sep ~15:20Z), flat ledgers:** 1.38 s for 234 decisions; 0 rows the table would refuse; S11/S12 no-trade band 108, nothing tradeable 7, against the market 2; no BUY. S10 was `no_ladder` in that test because its ladders come from the tick. **Part 3b next:** the BUY legs to the ledgers (an ENTER signal and `publish_paper_plan` per side, as paper_plans does), then shadow state. **After merge:** apply the migration live before the next tick, then check the first decisions rows and the tick's minute. **Live, 27 Sep 15:36Z (the first tick on #222): 13 city-days decided, 78 rows (NONE 60, WAIT 18), 0 written** (`ingest_log` job `tick`, `detail.engine.error` = HTTP 400 on `decisions`). The Postgres log at 15:36:39Z: `decisions_reason_code_check` refused a row. The check is `^[a-z_]{1,40}$` (no digits) and S10's own WAIT was coded `s10_wait`; one refused row fails the whole insert. The 39-checkpoint measurement above ran S10 without its ladders, so that path never met the constraint. **Fixed (this PR):** S10 declining by its own rule is `own_rule_<action>`; the test reads the pattern from the migration that declares it and runs S10 with a ladder (it fails on the old code with the live error). Checked on the live table in a block that always rolls back: the fixed rows were accepted (6 inserted), `s10_wait` was refused with the same `decisions_reason_code_check` error, and `decisions` still held 11,601 rows afterwards. **Check-in 27 Sep 16:45Z, the first tick on #224 (16:36Z):** 18 city-days x 6 strategies = 108 rows decided and written (`decisions` 11,601 -> 11,709); `detail.engine`: reached 18, out_of_time 0, 4.3 s, no error; actions NONE 78, WAIT 30; by code S10 `own_rule_wait` 30 and `no_ladder` 24, S11/S12 `no_trade_band` 52 and `nothing_tradeable` 2. Every row's `checkpoint_id` joins `prediction_checkpoints`; every S11/S12 `params_version` carries engine, market_anchor (w 0, prior) and clusters; S10 used its shadow ladder wherever `s10_shadow_checkpoints` had one (0 mismatches either way). The tick job ran 50 s (16:36:05-16:36:55Z; its Checkpoints step 24 s). **Found:** (1) S10 rows that decline by their own rule carry no `params_version`, though they read the market anchor: to record in part 3b. (2) The tick's trade prints (P0.4, moved into the tick) read nothing at 14:36, 15:36 and 16:36Z ("no time left before the tick's deadline", budget -2.6 / -3.1 / -3.9 s; last read 13:36Z, 739 trades): during 06-17Z the checkpoints, S10, the engine and the venue confirmations (11 s at 16:36Z) use the whole minute, and 14:36Z had no engine step yet. Part 3b's fills have to fit the same minute. (3) The tick logged "ok" at 15:36Z while the engine's insert failed; a failed engine step is now "attention" (`tick.py`, `tests/test_tick.py`, fails on the old code). **Part 3b, step 1 (27 Sep evening): S10 hears how old the station reading is.** Measured on `decisions`, 16:36-19:36Z: every S10 row with a ladder, 123 of 123, read `own_rule_wait` (and 27 read `no_ladder`). The cause: `engine_shadow` passed `reading_age_min = None`, and S10 waits on a reading it cannot date (`strategies.tradeable.readings_usable`: at most 75 min, the trajectory's own limit). The tick now hands the engine `v_city_running_max.latest_reading_at`, the newest reading of today's station series (ad4_71; the reading the trajectory stands on). The engine uses the reading, the floor and its basis only on the target's own local day, and only a reading taken before the decision. Engine rows 16:36-19:36Z: 0 BUY in 300; S11/S12 all `no_trade_band` / `nothing_tradeable` / `against_market`. **Found for the acceptance:** `backtest/replay_engine.py` also passes no floor and no reading age, so the replay's S10 cannot match live S10 rows until it reads the readings as of each decision (`data/replay/inputs_2026-09-26/obs_utc.csv.gz` has them). **Part 3b, step 2 (27 Sep evening): the BUYs reach the shadow ledgers, filled in the tick.** Mapped from the code and the live definitions first. `publish_paper_plan` needs an ENTER `signals` row, and writing one would also offer the decision to the old plan path (`paper_plans.cycle` takes any fresh ENTER of an enabled strategy in a ledger's policy). So migration `20260927210000` adds `publish_engine_plan(account, decision, legs, evidence)`. It needs a BUY decided in the last 15 min, by the ledger's own strategy, on a shadow ledger, for a switched-on strategy (`strategies.enabled`). There is one plan per decision (the command key is the decision's); `signal_id` is null (both columns nullable, checked), and `evidence.decision_id` names the decision. It calls `arbdesk_private.queue_plan`, so every rail, cap and holding check applies; a refusal leaves the plan `blocked` with the reason. A plan and its orders expire in 5 min, and a fill after that is refused (`complete_paper_order`), so the tick fills its own orders. `claim_account_order(account)` is `claim_paper_order` scoped to one ledger (the global one claims the first account by creation time, so the tick would fill other desks' queues). `paper_worker.fill_order` is extracted unchanged and is the one fill path. `scripts/engine_orders.py` sends the legs at the ask the tick read, with a reserve of shares x limit plus the fee at the worst price it can fill at, plus a cent. Legs under the venue minimum are left out and recorded. Measured for these priors: fee rate 0.05 and minimum order 5 USDC on all 1,729 books the paper engine captured 16-27 Sep; the worker averages 1.51 s per filled order (168 orders over 44 runs, 16-27 Sep). Ledgers fill in parallel, at most 12 fills a tick, none started with less than 2.5 s left. **Also in the migration:** the six engine ledgers were registered with `auto_exit_enabled` (15% stop, 25% gain), and `paper_exits` would have sold what the engine holds. They are now off, versioned and logged. `paper_positions.entry_decision_id` was declared uuid before `decisions` existed; it was empty (0 of 143) with no dependents, and is retyped to bigint. The entry trigger now records an engine fill's decision, its `params_version` and the engine's p at entry; every other order is recorded as before. **Harness:** its `paper_accounts` lacked production's `parent_account_id` during the migration phase (it comes from `sql/ad4_59`, applied after), so the first migration to update `paper_accounts` after the frozen-desk trigger failed there. The fixture now adds it right after the table's migration. **What goes on:** S11 and S12 (`decide` never sells: buy and hold to settlement, as designed). **S10 waits for step 3:** its own rules also SELL (certainly lost) and SWITCH, and this step sends BUYs only, so an S10 ledger now would record a no-switch variant rather than S10. **Part 3b, step 3 (27 Sep evening): S10 exits by its own rules.** S10 SELLs a held bucket the day's maximum has certainly ruled out, at the bid, and SWITCHes when the growth gained beats the exit cost and h_switch. `engine_views.engine_input` hands the engine S10's own decision (a trace), and for a SWITCH the buy half is built as a BUY of the new target is. `engine_shadow.exit_of` sizes that half with `decide` on the ledger as it will stand after the sale (`after_sale`: the proceeds at the net bid, the bucket's cost gone). When `decide` gives it no size (its band, a rail, the timing), the row is HOLD `switch_unsized`: selling would leave the ledger flat on a bucket S10 wanted to hold. Migration `20260927220000` adds `submit_engine_exit(account, decision, band, side, shares, limit)`: a SELL or SWITCH decided in the last 15 min by the ledger's own strategy, on a shadow ledger, for a switched-on strategy, never more than the position less what is already on its way out. It writes an automatic SELL order naming the decision in its context, one per decision, band and side, with 5 min to fill. `publish_engine_plan` also takes a SWITCH (its buy half). `record_paper_trade` closes an engine SELL's trades as `engine_exit`; every other SELL still closes as `auto_exit`. The tick (`engine_orders.send_exits`) sells first, one exit at a time, and fills each at once; a SWITCH's buy half is published only once its sale filled or partly filled, then goes through `queue_plan` like any BUY. Exits below the venue minimum at the bid (5 USDC) are not sent. `send` now expires leftover orders first (`expire_paper_commands`), and one refused exit or plan is counted with the database's reason instead of stopping the tick's other orders. Tests: `tests/test_engine_orders.py`, `tests/test_engine_shadow.py`, and the harness contract (refusals, the order, the claim, the fill, the `engine_exit` close, the SWITCH plan through `queue_plan`, a re-run changing nothing). **Live, 27 Sep:** migration `20260927220000` applied at 20:51:22Z (version 20260927205122); the three function bodies' md5s equal the file's; the `paper_accounts` and `paper_positions` fingerprints and the order, trade and plan counts (190, 164, 1,020) were identical before and after. `s10_growth` and `s10_lock` were switched on at 20:35:40Z through the web app (`set_strategy_enabled`, reason "strategies.enabled switched on"); `s10_winner` at 20:57:03Z (`set_strategy_state`). **Acceptance, part 1 (27 Sep night): the engine records what it decided on.** The plan's acceptance compares the replay with live `decisions` for 22 Sep; the first live engine row is from 27 Sep 16:36Z (measured on `decisions`), so that day cannot be compared, and the handoff (§8.3) restates it as one day. Read before building: the live engine decides on the tick's own `prediction_checkpoints` rows (the ladder and the book it read), S10 on `s10_shadow_checkpoints`, the floor, its basis and the newest reading from `v_city_running_max`, and the ledgers as read. The first two are kept; the floor's basis is not kept anywhere (the checkpoint row has the floor and the reading's time, not its basis, and S10's reading rule and every strategy's ruled-out test read it); the view is of now, and the ledgers change. So the tick now records, in its `ingest_log` detail (`engine.inputs`), each decided city's (date, floor, basis, reading time) and each ledger as read (cash, reserve, high water, today's P&L, positions and live BUY orders with their city-day). Also: an S10 row that declined by its own rule had no `params_version` (33 of 33 at 20:36Z, measured), though its ladder was anchored on the market; every S10 row now records the market anchor and S10's view version and h_switch (`engine_shadow.stamp_s10`). Tests: `tests/test_engine_shadow.py`. **And the comparison, nightly:** `scripts/engine_replay_live.py`, a step of `pipeline_daily` (04:36Z), reads the previous UTC day's ticks: each run's decisions, the checkpoint rows they name, S10's ladders of the model version the tick ran, and the recorded floors and ledgers. It replays each run through the same `engine_shadow.decide_all` and compares each row with live on (action, reason code), among rows decided on the same parameters: no key both rows record disagrees (an S10 row its own rule declined records fewer keys than one that reached `decide`, so a different decision must not read as different parameters; a fixture showed it would have). Learned tables are looked up by the version each row records; "prior" is none, as the loaders return while learning is off. It logs job `engine_replay` with the share matched (the plan's bar: 95%) and the first 30 mismatches; a run without recorded inputs is skipped and counted. Reads only; its minutes are not measured yet. The 390 rows stay: they are the old log's, told apart by `checkpoint_id` null. **Check-in 28 Sep 05:45Z:** nine ticks 21:36-05:36Z, all ok, 17.6-27.7 s (engine 2.4-5.3 s), no engine error, no order: 504 engine rows, every one NONE (S10 67 own_rule_none + 17 no_ladder each; s11_ladder 77 no_trade_band, 5 nothing_tradeable, 2 against_market; s11_lock 84 no_trade_band; s12_no 79 no_trade_band, 5 nothing_tradeable). The step took 6 s. Contract `tests/database/ladder-archive.cjs`: an unarchived tradeable ladder survives the prune at any age; the mark refuses without the verified count, on a count that moved, and under the one-day floor; it deletes nothing; after it the prune nulls exactly the stamped ones, never the newest per band; closed to the browser. | **Found 27 Sep:** `prune_dead_book_detail` (pg_cron hourly) nulled `raw_book`/`no_book` at 6 h (decided books) or 48 h (trading) without asking whether they were kept, and the `books` archive (7 days) always ran after: its 7 files hold 0 `raw_book` values. Of the LIVE snapshots of 12-24 Sep, 19 still carried a ladder; full ladders exist only from 25 Sep (2,294 of 5,043 LIVE that day, all 3,493 on 26 Sep). The plan's "taker fills use raw_book ladders, available from 12 Sep" (P5.12) is therefore no longer true: the replay of 12-24 Sep has the depth tiers (1/2/5/10/25c, `ad4_synth_levels`) and best bid/ask, not exact ladders. **Change:** archive dataset `ladders` (`scripts/archive_observations.py`, nightly with the others, no new schedule) exports `v_unarchived_ladders` older than a day, including `snapshot_id` (the edges archive's `book_snapshot_id` joins to it), commits and re-reads the file, then `mark_ladders_archived` stamps exactly those rows - a stamp, not a delete; the hourly prune now nulls a trading book's ladder only once stamped (migration `20260927100000_a_ladder_is_archived_before_it_is_pruned.sql`, and the same guard in `sql/ad4_prune_dead_book_detail.sql`). **Decided books** (DEAD_LOSER / DEAD_WINNER) keep the 6-hour prune and are not archived: ~500 an hour on 27 Sep (1,948 in four hours, 2.2 MB), a wall at 0.001 / 0.999, and held for a day they would add tens of MB to a database at 132% of its tier; their best bid/ask and tiers stay. Size: ~2-4 MB of ladder JSON a day before gzip (26 Sep: 3.8 MB LIVE, 0.4 MB WIDE and ONE_SIDED); gzipped size not measured until the first run. `web/public/archive/index.json` has no schema-version field, so there was none to bump; `edge_id` is not added to the edges archive (it pages on it and does not export it; `book_snapshot_id` is what joins a pricing to its ladder). **First run:** the 02:36Z archive of 28 Sep, which must run the merged code. |

What is fetched and every counter that decides `incomplete` (and so a paid continuation) are unchanged - `tests/test_ingest_forecasts_waits_in_parallel.py` drives the real `main()` with a simulated source: a city that hangs once is finished in the same run, one that never answers still leaves the run incomplete (asked twice, not forever), a refusal still counts as a missing chunk, and hangs overlap (4 workers under 0.6 x the time of 1). That exposed a bug: a cycle could only finish inside one run, so a backlog read one batch per hour would never have completed. **Fix (plan/p6-2-trades-cycle):** the saved position is a cursor (the last condition id done), and a cycle runs from the start of the sorted market list to its end across as many runs as it takes, keeping the older mark until it reaches the end. The batch number also shifted whenever a market opened: 1,045 markets at 09:05Z, 1,056 at 09:37Z. The 09:37Z row has no cursor, so its cycle restarts from the top with its mark; that is one batch read twice, with the duplicates ignored. **Next:** the clock into pg_cron + pg_net (-24 a day, needs the GitHub token in Supabase Vault as `github_actions_token` - asked of Hassan 25 Sep) and NWS P1.3/P1.4 into the tick (-8). Then ~41 a day, ~1,230 a month. **Earlier, 24 Sep (n8n keeps about 7 days of executions):** 101 executions in the last 24 h and 302 in the last 72 h, all workflows on the shared instance. That is about 100 a day, ~3,000 a month, before the P6.1 clock (+24 a day). This PR: `tick.py` reads every active station in ONE IEM request over the last 6 h (P1.6's window), with a 12 s timeout, before pricing. It uses `ingest_observations.fetch_station`/`parse`, the same table and key, and runs even when no checkpoint is due. A failed read marks the tick `attention` and never stops the checkpoints. P1.6 seeds `off` (`sql/ad4_20`; `sql/ad4_90` updates this database). P0.3's template fires every 2 h, with its gate at 110 min. After merge and a live tick that writes observations, P1.6 is unpublished in n8n (-720 a month) and P0.3's trigger goes to 2 h (-360). Projected from the measured rate: ~3,000 + 720 (clock) - 720 - 360 = **~2,640 a month, still over the 1,800 target.** Further levers, in the plan's direction (fold into the tick what the tick can do): P0.3 books into the tick (-360 more), and P1.2 NWS monitor (360 a month) and P1.5 Open-Meteo (240). The n8n clock (uzrfOy27RZcvoGaw) was unpublished at ~18:16Z, before its 18:36 slot. The Actions budget test now counts the clock from the migration's literal. **Verified live 26 Sep:** P6.1_clock at 18:36:00Z (clock supabase, dispatched tick.yml, request 3); P6.1_clock_check at 18:39:00Z ok (refused [], no_answer 0); GitHub tick run 36263116213, workflow_dispatch, 18:36:01-18:36:49Z, success; the n8n clock made 0 executions after 18:00Z. A one-off dispatch of forecasts.yml on a branch through the same pg_net path answered 204. **Still to measure:** n8n executions per day over a full day without the clock. **P1.2 in the tick, first runs checked 26 Sep:** `P1.2_nws_monitor` logged ok at 16:37, 18:36 and 20:36 (every other tick): 11 cities requested, 0 failed, 0 alerts, 640 NWS observations each, 7.5 / 5.2 / 8.2 s. `weather_observations` source NWS newest valid_at 20:25Z (501 readings after 16:30Z); `live_weather` NWS rows updated 20:36:28Z (12 rows). n8n P1.2 (tahTg5bLzFyeByqB) last ran 15:42Z - none at 17:42 or 19:42. Tick job 20:36:06-20:37:00 (54 s, one billed minute; NWS step 8 s). The trade-print step used 20.0 s: 3,039 prints fetched, 2,139 new, 8 of 9 batches, cursor saved. **27 Sep check-in:** since 26 Sep 18:36Z every `P6.1_clock` row is clock=supabase and ok (13 of 13, to 06:36Z), every `P6.1_clock_check` ok (12 of 12), and every tick ran once an hour and ok (12 to 05:36Z, plus 06:36Z); tick.yml's 7 runs on 27 Sep to 06:36Z each billed 1 minute (35-52 s). The n8n clock workflow was not queried for executions here - not verified. **28 Sep: the trade prints were starved by the tick's deadline, and now run beside it.** tick.yml ran `ingest_trades.py` last, on what the other steps left before TICK_DEADLINE (48 s). `ingest_log` 27 Sep 16:36Z-28 Sep 19:36Z: tick.py took 17.6-33.1 s, the NWS readings 5.5-8.7 s on even hours, and the venue confirmations run 06-17Z with a 20 s backstop, so the trade step's budget was <= 0 in 9 of 27 ticks and under 5 s in 6 more. On 28 Sep 11 of its 19 runs wrote 0 trades, the cycle stood at its 04:36Z mark all day, and 28 Sep held 11,238 trades at 19:19Z against 37,759 on 27 Sep and 56,510 on 26 Sep. Nothing was lost: the mark held (plan()), 0 of 83 runs since 25 Sep truncated a batch and 0 had an error, so the next runs read the gap. **Fix:** tick.yml starts the step in the background before the checkpoints with `--budget 30`, and the last step collects its log and exit code; `budget()` still ends it before the same deadline, so the job stays one billed minute (the step waits on the API, not the CPU). Tests: the order and the backgrounding in tick.yml, 30 s inside the 48 s deadline, and the two steps run under the runner's own `bash -eo pipefail` with a fake run that exits 3 (the start returns at once, the last step fails with 3). **Acceptance, pending the first ticks after merge:** the trade step's budget_s near 30 in every tick, the cycle completing (complete: true) within a few hours, 28 Sep's count rising, and tick.yml still billing 1 minute a run. **Measured on the 20:36Z tick, the first on #253 (merged as 294a78d):** the job ran 38 s (20:36:06-20:36:44, one billed minute); the start step took under a second and the collect step waited 5 s and passed the exit code; the trade step had budget 30.0 s, used 29.8 s, read 3 of the 6 batches left in its cycle and wrote 11,907 new trades; tick.py took 18.8 s (17.6-33.1 s the day before). 28 Sep rose from 15,344 trades (~19:55Z) to 27,251. The cycle, still on its 04:36Z mark, finishes on the next ticks. **29 Sep:** the trade step beside the tick had budget 30 s on every tick from 20:36Z; the cycle completed at 21:36Z (3 of 3 batches) and on every tick after, through 03:36Z and 05:37Z. At 04:36Z and 06:36Z its first request got HTTP 401 from Supabase and the job ended `failure` (P5.12 notes); the next tick resumed from the mark. |

Model vs floor+forecast: +0.248 to +0.314 C, every 95% interval above +0.23. Already set at the hour: 7% (09h), 14%, 41%, 77% (15h). At 15h the error-shifted forecast beats the ridge: the two-part model (P(set) and the rise) must keep it as an input. **Not yet compared with the engine's own same-day prices** (only ~2 weeks of frozen checkpoints) nor scored as bucket probabilities; both are the stage 1 acceptance. **Stage 1, first cut (`tools/experiments_p72_stage1.py`)**, as a distribution on the whole-degree bucket (P(max already set) logistic + the rise lognormal with a state-dependent scale) against a floor-atom Gaussian around floor+forecast: log loss 09h 1.999 vs 2.016 (+0.017 [-0.001, 0.032]), 11h 1.713 vs 1.898 (+0.184 [0.171, 0.197]), 13h 1.262 vs 1.608 (+0.346 [0.328, 0.364]), 15h 0.641 vs 1.357 (+0.716 [0.690, 0.746]); most-likely-bucket hit 25.2/24.7%, 32.3/26.5%, 49.1/41.6%, 77.6/61.8%. The 09h distribution needs work (its hit is below the point ridge's 31.3%). **Stage 1 (26 Sep, late), `scripts/remaining_day.py`, scored through the module itself by `tools/experiments_p72_stage1.py`** (walk-forward by month, Nov 2025-Sep 2026, each month fitted only on days before it, 48 cities, 329 days, 11 fits per hour; labels are WHOLE days only - the first measurements' labels held 43 partial rows, and re-running them without those rows moved no number by more than 0.002). The form: an equal mix of A (P(max already set) logistic + a lognormal rise) and B (the floor atom around a ridge's expected rise), each with a day-dependent scale, per decision hour, pooled across cities with each city's coefficients shrunk to the pool (MIN_CITY_ROWS 60), plus the seven models' day-ahead spread as a feature. Chosen on the same months among four forms (scratch, numpy; bucket log loss 09/11/13/15h): A 1.924/1.669/1.290/0.700, B 1.805/1.644/1.298/0.741, mix 1.801/1.616/1.257/0.684; without city effects B was 1.882 at 09h - so the mix's edge over the better single form is not a confirmed result. **Against the floor-atom Gaussian around max(R, forecast day max)** (log loss on the whole degree in the city's unit / most-likely-degree hit / CRPS C): 09h 1.801 vs 2.048, gain +0.247 [0.230, 0.260], hit 32.0% vs 25.3%, CRPS 0.741 vs 0.899; 11h 1.616 vs 1.945, +0.329 [0.314, 0.345], 37.0% vs 26.8%, 0.616 vs 0.812; 13h 1.255 vs 1.720, +0.464 [0.442, 0.493], 51.1% vs 41.7%, 0.449 vs 0.661; 15h 0.680 vs 1.604, +0.924 [0.885, 0.962], 78.3% vs 61.6%, 0.250 vs 0.538 (n 15,717-15,731 per hour). The numpy scratch model gave 1.801/1.616/1.257/0.684: the pure-Python module is the same model. **Heteroscedastic, but under-covering at midday.** Width of the 80% interval by tercile of the models' disagreement (low/mid/high): 09h 2.74/3.14/3.86 C, 11h 2.15/2.49/3.03, 13h 1.56/1.72/1.92, 15h 0.77/0.79/0.81 - wider when the models disagree, as the plan requires. Coverage before calibration: 09h 80.3/78.4/77.6%, 15h 79.4/79.9/80.0%, but 11h 75.7/75.5/75.3% and 13h 71.3/70.8/71.9% - the plan's 80%-coverage test was not met there. Diagnosed on Jun-Aug at 13h (fit on the days before): 17.3% of maxima fall below the 10th percentile and 11.4% above the 90th; labels below R are only 2.3%, so the model puts too little mass near R at midday. Tests (`tests/test_remaining_day.py`): no feature reads a reading after the decision hour, features are pure, the ladder sums to 1 with the P3.1 atom (nothing below b_R, q_down only one bucket down), F edges on the whole-degree lattice, a CDF that is zero below R and monotone, the forecast dominating an uninformative morning, a city leaving the pool only with enough days, every output bounded, a version on every fit.  **Calibrated width (`choose_widen`)**: both scales times the smallest factor in 1.0-1.6 whose 80% interval covers >= 80% of the training window's last fifth, fitted on the days before it (Rule 11: prior 1.0, MIN_INNER_ROWS 400). Chosen per month: 09h 1.05-1.2, 11h 1.15-1.4, 13h 1.3-1.6, 15h 1.0-1.2. Same walk-forward after: coverage low/mid/high 09h 83.5/81.7/81.1%, 11h 83.1/82.2/82.3%, 13h 84.0/82.9/83.1%, 15h 81.4/82.0/82.3% - **met at every hour and tercile**; width 09h 2.98/3.42/4.25 C, 13h 1.94/2.15/2.42 C. Cost in bucket log loss: 09h 1.804 (+0.003), 11h 1.627 (+0.011), 13h 1.269 (+0.014), 15h 0.678 (-0.002); against the baseline +0.244 [0.229, 0.258], +0.318 [0.304, 0.333], +0.451 [0.430, 0.478], +0.926 [0.888, 0.964]; hit 31.8/36.6/50.7/78.2%. ### 23 Sep 2026 — outside the plan: one temperature per Celsius bucket
- **Hassan:** Predictive → a city → "Hit and miss, day by day" showed Celsius calls as a range ("22-23"). It should show one temperature. Only °F buckets are ranges ("70-71°F").
- **Cause:** `v_city_hit_history` built its label from the stored edges. A band is half-open [lo, hi), so the single degree 22 has the edges 22 and 23.
- **Fix:** the view takes the venue's own `band_label` from `v_canonical_bands`, with a half-open fallback. Applied live on 23 Sep in one transaction:
  - 383 rows before and after;
  - hit flags, Brier scores, p on the winner and errors are identical (EXCEPT ALL, 0 rows both ways);
  - 0 Celsius ranges are left;
  - anon reads 294 °C rows (e.g. "28°C / 27°C") and 89 °F rows (e.g. "68-69°F / 64-65°F"), and `v_city_hit_summary` still returns 49 cities.
- **Canonical ladder (measured the same day):** closed °C buckets are 1 wide with single-degree labels (12,582 bands). Closed °F buckets are 2 wide with range labels (3,869 bands).
- **Test:** `tests/test_a_celsius_bucket_is_one_degree.py`.
